Notations and Preliminaries

Throughout the paper, if not further specified, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega $$\end{document}Ω denotes a bounded, open and convex subset of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {R}^{n}$$\end{document}Rn, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$n\ge 2$$\end{document}n≥2 and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega _T:=\Omega \times (0,T)$$\end{document}ΩT:=Ω×(0,T) is the associated space-time cylinder, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$T>0$$\end{document}T>0. The parabolic boundary of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega _{T}$$\end{document}ΩT is defined by\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \partial _{\mathcal {P}}\Omega _{T} := \left( \overline{\Omega }\times \{0\}\right) \cup \left( \partial \Omega \times (0,T)\right) . $$\end{document}∂PΩT:=Ω¯×{0}∪∂Ω×(0,T).As already mentioned, we frequently use the identification of the class of Lipschitz continuous functions \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C^{0,1}(\overline{\Omega })$$\end{document}C0,1(Ω¯) with the space \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$W^{1,\infty }(\Omega )$$\end{document}W1,∞(Ω). For a continuous function \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u:\overline{\Omega }\rightarrow \mathbb {R}$$\end{document}u:Ω¯→R we recall the definition of the Lipschitz constant\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ {[}u]_{0,1;\Omega }:=\sup _{x\not =y, x,y\in \Omega }\frac{|u(x)-u(y)|}{|x-y|}<\infty . $$\end{document}[u]0,1;Ω:=supx≠y,x,y∈Ω|u(x)-u(y)||x-y|<∞.Since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega $$\end{document}Ω is convex, we have that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$[u]_{0,1;\Omega }=\Vert \nabla u\Vert _{L^\infty (\Omega ,\mathbb {R}^n)}$$\end{document}[u]0,1;Ω=‖∇u‖L∞(Ω,Rn).

R-Uniform Convexity and Time Dependent Bounded Slope Condition

In this subsection we specify the notion of R-uniform convexity and the time dependent bounded slope condition which have already been used in the statement of Theorem 1.3.

Definition 2.1

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$R>0$$\end{document}R>0. A subset \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega \subset \mathbb {R}^n$$\end{document}Ω⊂Rn is called R-uniformly convex, if for any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$x_o\in \partial \Omega $$\end{document}xo∈∂Ω there exists a unit vector \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\nu _{x_o}\in \mathbb {R}^n$$\end{document}νxo∈Rn pointing outside of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega $$\end{document}Ω such that2.1\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} R\nu _{x_o}\cdot (x-x_o)\le - \tfrac{1}{2} |x-x_o|^2 \qquad \text{ for } \text{ any } x\in \partial \Omega . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Rνxo·(x-xo)≤-12|x-xo|2foranyx∈∂Ω.

Remark 2.2

The notion of R-uniform convexity implies that for any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$x\in \partial \Omega $$\end{document}x∈∂Ω there holds\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} |x-(x_o-R\nu _{x_o})|^2&= |x-x_o|^2 +2R\nu _{x_o}\cdot (x-x_o)+R^2\le R^2. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}|x-(xo-Rνxo)|2=|x-xo|2+2Rνxo·(x-xo)+R2≤R2.For the last inequality we used that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\nu _{x_o}$$\end{document}νxo points out of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega $$\end{document}Ω and (2.1). In particular, we have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega \subset B_R(x_o-R\nu _{x_o})$$\end{document}Ω⊂BR(xo-Rνxo).

Since the boundary values g depend on time, we have to introduce a time dependent version of the bounded slope condition.

Definition 2.3

A function \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$g:\partial \Omega \times [0,T]\rightarrow \mathbb {R}$$\end{document}g:∂Ω×[0,T]→R satisfies the time dependent bounded slope condition with constant \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Q>0$$\end{document}Q>0 (hereafter abbreviated as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$t-\textrm{BSC}_Q$$\end{document}t-BSCQ), if for each \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$x_o\in \partial \Omega $$\end{document}xo∈∂Ω there exist functions \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$w_{x_o}^\pm :[0,T]\rightarrow \mathbb {R}^n$$\end{document}wxo±:[0,T]→Rn satisfying \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert w_{x_o}^{\pm }\Vert _{L^\infty ([0,T],\mathbb {R}^n)}\le Q$$\end{document}‖wxo±‖L∞([0,T],Rn)≤Q such that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ g(x_o,t)+w_{x_o}^-(t)\cdot (x-x_o) \le g(x,t) \le g(x_o,t)+w_{x_o}^+(t)\cdot (x-x_o) $$\end{document}g(xo,t)+wxo-(t)·(x-xo)≤g(x,t)≤g(xo,t)+wxo+(t)·(x-xo)for any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(x,t)\in \partial \Omega \times [0,T]$$\end{document}(x,t)∈∂Ω×[0,T].

Remark 2.4

In the framework of stationary problems Miranda [22, 36] proved that if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega $$\end{document}Ω is a uniformly convex, bounded domain and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v\in C^2(\overline{\Omega })$$\end{document}v∈C2(Ω¯), then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v|_{\partial \Omega }$$\end{document}v|∂Ω satisfies the bounded slope condition. In the time dependent setting it follows that if g(x, t) is a \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C^{2}(\overline{\Omega _T})$$\end{document}C2(ΩT¯) function, then it satisfies the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$t-\textrm{BSC}_Q$$\end{document}t-BSCQ for some \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Q>0$$\end{document}Q>0.

The time dependent bounded slope condition from Definition 2.3 involves a function g which is defined only on the lateral boundary of the parabolic cylinder. In the next lemma we will see an application for a function G defined on the whole parabolic cylinder and whose boundary values satisfy the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$t-\textrm{BSC}_Q$$\end{document}t-BSCQ.

Lemma 2.5

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G:\Omega \times [0,T]\rightarrow \mathbb {R}$$\end{document}G:Ω×[0,T]→R with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sup _{t\in [0,T]}\textrm{Lip}^xG(\cdot ,t)\le Q_o$$\end{document}supt∈[0,T]LipxG(·,t)≤Qo such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$g:=G\big \vert _{\partial \Omega \times [0,T]}$$\end{document}g:=G|∂Ω×[0,T] satisfies the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$t-\textrm{BSC}_{Q}$$\end{document}t-BSCQ for some constant Q. Then for every \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$x_o\in \partial \Omega $$\end{document}xo∈∂Ω there exist \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widetilde{w}_{x_o}^\pm :[0,T]\rightarrow \mathbb {R}^n$$\end{document}w~xo±:[0,T]→Rn with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert \widetilde{w}_{x_o}^\pm \Vert _{L^\infty ([0,T],\mathbb {R}^n)}\le Q + Q_o$$\end{document}‖w~xo±‖L∞([0,T],Rn)≤Q+Qo such that for any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(x,t)\in \overline{\Omega }\times [0,T]$$\end{document}(x,t)∈Ω¯×[0,T] we have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} g(x_o,t)+\widetilde{w}_{x_o}^{-}(t)\cdot (x-x_o) \le G(x,t) \le g(x_o,t)+\widetilde{w}_{x_o}^{+}(t)\cdot (x-x_o). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}g(xo,t)+w~xo-(t)·(x-xo)≤G(x,t)≤g(xo,t)+w~xo+(t)·(x-xo).If we additionally assume that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$w_{x_o}^\pm \in W^{1,\infty }([0,T],\mathbb {R}^n)$$\end{document}wxo±∈W1,∞([0,T],Rn), then\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \Vert (\widetilde{w}_{x_o}^\pm )'\Vert _{L^\infty ([0,T],\mathbb {R}^n)}=\Vert (w_{x_o}^\pm )'\Vert _{L^\infty ([0,T],\mathbb {R}^n)}. $$\end{document}‖(w~xo±)′‖L∞([0,T],Rn)=‖(wxo±)′‖L∞([0,T],Rn).

Proof

Fix a boundary point \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$x_o\in \partial \Omega $$\end{document}xo∈∂Ω and denote by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$[0,T]\ni t\mapsto w_{x_o}^{+}(t)$$\end{document}[0,T]∋t↦wxo+(t) the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {R}^n$$\end{document}Rn-valued function from the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$t-\textrm{BSC}_{Q}$$\end{document}t-BSCQ satisfied by g. Recall that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|w_{x_o}^{+}(t)|\le Q$$\end{document}|wxo+(t)|≤Q and\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} g(x,t)\le g(x_o,t)+w_{x_o}^{+}(t)\cdot (x-x_o) \end{aligned}$$\end{document}g(x,t)≤g(xo,t)+wxo+(t)·(x-xo)for any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$x\in \partial \Omega $$\end{document}x∈∂Ω and any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$t\in [0,T]$$\end{document}t∈[0,T]. Next, we fix a time \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$t\in [0,T]$$\end{document}t∈[0,T]. Without loss of generality we assume that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$x_o=0$$\end{document}xo=0, that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$g(x_o,t)=0$$\end{document}g(xo,t)=0, and that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega \subset \mathbb {R}^{n-1}\times \mathbb {R}_+$$\end{document}Ω⊂Rn-1×R+. Consider \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$x=(x',x_n)\in \Omega $$\end{document}x=(x′,xn)∈Ω. By \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(x',y_n)\in \partial \Omega $$\end{document}(x′,yn)∈∂Ω we denote the unique point in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial \Omega $$\end{document}∂Ω with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0\le y_n <x_n$$\end{document}0≤yn<xn. Then, we obtain\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} G(x,t)&\le G(x',x_n,t)-g(x',y_n,t)+ w_{x_o}^+(t)\cdot (x',y_n)\\&\le Q_o (x_n-y_n)+w_{x_o}^+(t)\cdot (x',y_n)\\&\le Q_o (x_n-y_n)+(w_{x_o}^+(t))'\cdot x'+(w_{x_o}^+(t))_n y_n\\&\le Q_o x_n+(w_{x_o}^+(t))'\cdot x'+|w_{x_o}^+(t))_n| x_n\\&\le \big [\underbrace{Q_oe_n+ \overline{w}_{x_o}^+(t)}_{=:\,\widetilde{w}_{x_o}^+(t)}\big ]\cdot (x',x_n), \end{aligned}$$\end{document}G(x,t)≤G(x′,xn,t)-g(x′,yn,t)+wxo+(t)·(x′,yn)≤Qo(xn-yn)+wxo+(t)·(x′,yn)≤Qo(xn-yn)+(wxo+(t))′·x′+(wxo+(t))nyn≤Qoxn+(wxo+(t))′·x′+|wxo+(t))n|xn≤[Qoen+w¯xo+(t)⏟=:w~xo+(t)]·(x′,xn),where we used that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0\le y_n<x_n$$\end{document}0≤yn<xn and we defined \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\overline{w}_{x_o}^+(t))=(w_{x_o}^+(t))',|w_{x_o}^+(t))_n|)$$\end{document}w¯xo+(t))=(wxo+(t))′,|wxo+(t))n|). Observing that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|\overline{w}_{x_o}^+(t))|=|w_{x_o}^+(t))|\le Q$$\end{document}|w¯xo+(t))|=|wxo+(t))|≤Q we have proven that g satisfies the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$t-\textrm{BSC}_{Q_1}$$\end{document}t-BSCQ1 with the larger constant \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Q_1=Q+Q_o$$\end{document}Q1=Q+Qo. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

Mollification in Time

In the definition of variational solutions we do not impose any condition on the time derivative. Therefore, in general we cannot insert the variational solution itself as comparison map in the variational inequality (1.4). Hence, a suitable mollification procedure in time is needed. For a separable Banach space X, an initial datum \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v_{o}\in X$$\end{document}vo∈X and an integrability exponent \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$1\le r\le \infty $$\end{document}1≤r≤∞, we consider \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v\in L^{r}(0,T;X)$$\end{document}v∈Lr(0,T;X) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$h\in (0,T]$$\end{document}h∈(0,T] and define the mollification in time by2.2\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} {[}v]_{h}(t):=\textrm{e}^{-\frac{t}{h}}v_{o} +\tfrac{1}{h}\int _{0}^{t}\textrm{e}^{\frac{s-t}{h}}v(s)\,\textrm{d}s, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}[v]h(t):=e-thvo+1h∫0tes-thv(s)ds,whenever \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$t\in [0,T]$$\end{document}t∈[0,T]. Later on, we use for instance \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$X=L^{r}(\Omega ,\mathbb {R}^{N})$$\end{document}X=Lr(Ω,RN) or \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$X=W^{1,r}(\Omega ,\mathbb {R}^{N})$$\end{document}X=W1,r(Ω,RN) and the related parabolic spaces \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^{r}(0,T;L^{r}(\Omega ,\mathbb {R}^{N}))$$\end{document}Lr(0,T;Lr(Ω,RN)), respectively \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^{r}(0,T;W^{1,r}(\Omega ,\mathbb {R}^{N}))$$\end{document}Lr(0,T;W1,r(Ω,RN)). One particular feature of this mollification is that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\left[ v\right] _{h}$$\end{document}vh solves the ordinary differential equation\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \partial _{t}[v]_{h}=\tfrac{1}{h}\big (v-[v]_{h}\big ) \end{aligned}$$\end{document}∂t[v]h=1h(v-[v]h)with initial condition \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$[v]_{h}(0)=v_{o}$$\end{document}[v]h(0)=vo. Note that, since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$[v]_{h}$$\end{document}[v]h solves the above ODE, then clearly any common membership of both v and its regularization \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$[v]_{h}$$\end{document}[v]h to a Banach space is passed to the time derivative \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial _t [v]_{h}$$\end{document}∂t[v]h. The basic properties of the mollification in time are summarized in the following lemma (cf. [29, Lemma 2.2] and [3, Appendix B] for the proofs).

Lemma 2.6

Suppose X is a separable Banach space and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v_{o}\in X$$\end{document}vo∈X. Furthermore, for any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$t_{o}\in (0,T]$$\end{document}to∈(0,T] it holds that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \left\| [v]_{h}\right\| _{L^{r}\left( 0,t_{o};X\right) } \le \left\| v\right\| _{L^{r}\left( 0,t_{o};X\right) } + \left[ \tfrac{h}{r}\left( 1-\textrm{e}^{-\frac{t_{o}r}{h}}\right) \right] ^{\frac{1}{r}} \left\| v_{o}\right\| _{X}. $$\end{document}[v]hLr0,to;X≤vLr0,to;X+hr1-e-torh1rvoX.In the case \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$r=\infty $$\end{document}r=∞, the bracket \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\left[ \ldots \right] ^{1/r}$$\end{document}…1/r in the preceding inequality has to be interpreted as 1. Moreover, we have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial _{t}[v]_{h}\in L^{r}\left( 0,T;X\right) $$\end{document}∂t[v]h∈Lr0,T;X with2.3\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} Properties of Variational Solutions

In this section we present some properties of variational solutions that are direct consequences of the definition. These properties can be achieved in a fairly general setting, in particular without assuming the bounded slope condition.

Lemma 3.1

Assume that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$f:\mathbb {R}^n\rightarrow \mathbb {R}$$\end{document}f:Rn→R is convex and bounded from below and g satisfies (1.2). Then, any variational solution u in the sense of Definition 1.1, resp. 1.2 fulfills the initial condition \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u(0) = g_o$$\end{document}u(0)=go in the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^2$$\end{document}L2-sense, i.e. we have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \lim _{h\downarrow 0} \tfrac{1}{h}\int _0^h\Vert (g-u)(\tau )\Vert _{L^{2}(\Omega )}^{2}\,\textrm{d}\tau =0. $$\end{document}limh↓01h∫0h‖(g-u)(τ)‖L2(Ω)2dτ=0.

Proof

In view of assumption (1.2) we find \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L>0$$\end{document}L>0 satisfying (1.5). Since g is an admissible comparison map in (1.4), resp. (1.6), we have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \tfrac{1}{2}\Vert (g-u)(\tau )\Vert _{L^{2}(\Omega )}^{2} + \iint _{\Omega _\tau }f(\nabla u)\,\textrm{d}x\textrm{d}t&\le \iint _{\Omega _\tau }\big [\partial _{t}g(g-u) + f(\nabla g)\big ]\,\textrm{d}x\textrm{d}t\\&\le \iint _{\Omega _\tau }|\partial _{t}g||g-u|\,\textrm{d}x\textrm{d}t+ \tau |\Omega | \sup _{|\xi |\le L} f(\xi ) , \end{aligned}$$\end{document}12‖(g-u)(τ)‖L2(Ω)2+∬Ωτf(∇u)dxdt≤∬Ωτ[∂tg(g-u)+f(∇g)]dxdt≤∬Ωτ|∂tg||g-u|dxdt+τ|Ω|sup|ξ|≤Lf(ξ),for a.e. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tau \in [0,T]$$\end{document}τ∈[0,T]. On the left-hand side we discard the non-negative energy term. Then, we integrate for given \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$h>0$$\end{document}h>0 with respect to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tau $$\end{document}τ over the interval [0, h]. In this way we get\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \tfrac{1}{2}\int _0^h&\Vert (g-u)(\tau )\Vert _{L^{2}(\Omega )}^{2}\,\textrm{d}\tau \\&\le h\iint _{\Omega _h}|\partial _{t}g||g-u|\,\textrm{d}x\textrm{d}t+ \tfrac{1}{2} h^2|\Omega | \bigg [\sup _{|\xi |\le L} f(\xi ) + \Big |\inf _{\mathbb {R}^n} f\Big |\bigg ]. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}12∫0h‖(g-u)(τ)‖L2(Ω)2dτ≤h∬Ωh|∂tg||g-u|dxdt+12h2|Ω|[sup|ξ|≤Lf(ξ)+|infRnf|].We divide both sides by h and apply Young’s inequality to the first term on the right. This gives\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \tfrac{1}{h}\int _0^h&\Vert (g-u)(\tau )\Vert _{L^{2}(\Omega )}^{2}\,\textrm{d}\tau \\&\le \iint _{\Omega _h} \big [|\partial _{t}g|^2+|g-u|^2\big ]\, \textrm{d}x\textrm{d}t+ h|\Omega | \bigg [\sup _{|\xi |\le L} f(\xi ) + \Big |\inf _{\mathbb {R}^n} f\Big |\bigg ]. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}1h∫0h‖(g-u)(τ)‖L2(Ω)2dτ≤∬Ωh[|∂tg|2+|g-u|2]dxdt+h|Ω|[sup|ξ|≤Lf(ξ)+|infRnf|].Note that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial _tg\in L^2(\Omega _T)$$\end{document}∂tg∈L2(ΩT) implies \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$g\in C^0( [0,T];L^2(\Omega ))$$\end{document}g∈C0([0,T];L2(Ω)). As the right-hand side vanishes in the limit \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$h\downarrow 0$$\end{document}h↓0 and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$g(0)=g_o$$\end{document}g(0)=go, the claim follows. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

In the next lemma we show that variational solutions in the sense of Definition 1.1 apriori are of class \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C^0( [0,T]; L^2(\Omega ))$$\end{document}C0([0,T];L2(Ω)). The argument is similar to [9, Lemma 3.2]. Here, however, we have to estimate the difference of energy terms in a different way.

Lemma 3.2

Assume that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$f:\mathbb {R}^n\rightarrow \mathbb {R}$$\end{document}f:Rn→R is convex and g satisfies (1.2). Then, every variational solution u in the sense of Definition 1.1 satisfies\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ u\in C^0\big ( [0,T]; L^2(\Omega )\big ). $$\end{document}u∈C0([0,T];L2(Ω)).

Proof

We test the variational inequality for u with\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} v_h= g+[u-g]_h =g+\tfrac{1}{h}\int _0^t\mathrm e^\frac{s-t}{h}\big (u(s)-g(s)\big )\,\textrm{d}s, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}vh=g+[u-g]h=g+1h∫0tes-th(u(s)-g(s))ds,where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$[u-g]_h$$\end{document}[u-g]h is defined as in (2.2) with initial value \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v_o=0$$\end{document}vo=0. In view of Lemma 2.6, one easily shows \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v_h\in \textrm{Lip}^x_{g}(\Omega _T)$$\end{document}vh∈Lipgx(ΩT), \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial _t v_h\in L^2(\Omega _T)$$\end{document}∂tvh∈L2(ΩT), and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v_h(0)=g_o\in L^2(\Omega )$$\end{document}vh(0)=go∈L2(Ω), so that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v_h$$\end{document}vh is admissible in (1.4). In particular, we have a uniform bound for the spatial gradient \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|\nabla v_h|$$\end{document}|∇vh| in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega _T$$\end{document}ΩT. In fact, using the definition of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v_h$$\end{document}vh and assumption (1.2) we get\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} |\nabla v_h|&= \bigg |\nabla g +\tfrac{1}{h}\int _0^t\mathrm e^\frac{s-t}{h}\big (\nabla u(s)-\nabla g(s)\big )\,\textrm{d}s\bigg |\\&\le L+ \big (\Vert \nabla u\Vert _{L^\infty (\Omega _T,\mathbb {R}^n)}{+}L\big ) \tfrac{1}{h}\int _0^t\mathrm e^\frac{s-t}{h}\textrm{d}s\\&\le 2L+ \Vert \nabla u\Vert _{L^\infty (\Omega _T,\mathbb {R}^n)}=:\widetilde{L}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}|∇vh|=|∇g+1h∫0tes-th(∇u(s)-∇g(s))ds|≤L+(‖∇u‖L∞(ΩT,Rn)+L)1h∫0tes-thds≤2L+‖∇u‖L∞(ΩT,Rn)=:L~.Testing the variational inequality (1.4) with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v_h$$\end{document}vh we obtain\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \tfrac{1}{2}\Vert (v_h-u)(\tau )\Vert _{L^{2}(\Omega )}^{2}&\le \iint _{\Omega _\tau }\partial _{t}v_h(v_h-u)\,\textrm{d}x\textrm{d}t+ \iint _{\Omega _\tau } \big [f(\nabla v_h)- f(\nabla u)\big ]\,\textrm{d}x\textrm{d}t\end{aligned}$$\end{document}12‖(vh-u)(τ)‖L2(Ω)2≤∬Ωτ∂tvh(vh-u)dxdt+∬Ωτ[f(∇vh)-f(∇u)]dxdtfor a.e. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tau \in [0,T]$$\end{document}τ∈[0,T]. In the last displayed inequality we pass to the supremum over \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tau \in (0,T)$$\end{document}τ∈(0,T). This implies\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \tfrac{1}{2}\sup _{\tau \in (0,T)}&\Vert (v_h-u)(\tau )\Vert _{L^{2}(\Omega )}^{2}\\&\le \sup _{\tau \in (0,T)} \iint _{\Omega _\tau }\partial _{t}v_h(v_h-u)\,\textrm{d}x\textrm{d}t+ \iint _{\Omega _T} \big | f(\nabla v_h)- f(\nabla u)\big |\,\textrm{d}x\textrm{d}t. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}12supτ∈(0,T)‖(vh-u)(τ)‖L2(Ω)2≤supτ∈(0,T)∬Ωτ∂tvh(vh-u)dxdt+∬ΩT|f(∇vh)-f(∇u)|dxdt.The second term on the right-hand side can be bounded by means of the gradient bound for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|\nabla v_h|$$\end{document}|∇vh| in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega _T$$\end{document}ΩT. Indeed, we get\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \big | f(\nabla v_h)- f(\nabla u)\big |&\le \sup _{|\xi |\le \widetilde{L}} |\nabla f(\xi )| | \nabla v_h-\nabla u| \rightarrow 0\quad \text{ in } L^1(\Omega _T) \text{ as } h\downarrow 0, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}|f(∇vh)-f(∇u)|≤sup|ξ|≤L~|∇f(ξ)||∇vh-∇u|→0inL1(ΩT)ash↓0,so that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \lim _{h\downarrow 0}\iint _{\Omega _T} \big | f(\nabla v_h)- f(\nabla u)\big |\,\textrm{d}x\textrm{d}t=0. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}limh↓0∬ΩT|f(∇vh)-f(∇u)|dxdt=0.Moreover, exploiting (2.3), we infer that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}&\iint _{\Omega _\tau }\partial _{t}v_h(v_h-u)\,\textrm{d}x\textrm{d}t\\&\qquad = \iint _{\Omega _\tau }\Big [\partial _{t}g\big (g-u+[u-g]_h\big ) + \partial _{t}[u-g]_h \big (g-u+[u-g]_h\big )\Big ] \,\textrm{d}x\textrm{d}t\\&\qquad \le \iint _{\Omega _\tau } |\partial _{t}g| \big |u-g-[u-g]_h\big | \,\textrm{d}x\textrm{d}t. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}∬Ωτ∂tvh(vh-u)dxdt=∬Ωτ[∂tg(g-u+[u-g]h)+∂t[u-g]h(g-u+[u-g]h)]dxdt≤∬Ωτ|∂tg||u-g-[u-g]h|dxdt.so that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \limsup _{h\downarrow 0} \sup _{\tau \in (0,T)} \iint _{\Omega _\tau }\partial _{t}v_h(v_h-u)\textrm{d}x\textrm{d}t\le 0, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}lim suph↓0supτ∈(0,T)∬Ωτ∂tvh(vh-u)dxdt≤0,Inserting the preceding estimates above, we arrive at\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \lim _{h\downarrow 0} \Vert v_h-u\Vert _{L^\infty (0,T; L^2(\Omega ))}=0. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}limh↓0‖vh-u‖L∞(0,T;L2(Ω))=0.Note that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$g\in C^0( [0,T];L^2(\Omega ))$$\end{document}g∈C0([0,T];L2(Ω)), since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial _tg\in L^2(\Omega _T)$$\end{document}∂tg∈L2(ΩT). This however means that also \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v_h$$\end{document}vh belongs to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C^0( [0,T];L^2(\Omega ))$$\end{document}C0([0,T];L2(Ω)). Thus \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u\in L^\infty (0,T; L^2(\Omega ))$$\end{document}u∈L∞(0,T;L2(Ω)) is the limit with respect to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^\infty ( 0,T; L^2(\Omega ))$$\end{document}L∞(0,T;L2(Ω))-convergence of functions \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v_h$$\end{document}vh which are themselves of class \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C^0( [0,T];L^2(\Omega ))$$\end{document}C0([0,T];L2(Ω)). As a result the limit map u is continuous with respect to time, i.e. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u\in C^0( [0,T];L^2(\Omega ))$$\end{document}u∈C0([0,T];L2(Ω)). This proves the claim. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

Existence of Solutions for Regularized Boundary Data

In this section we establish for sufficiently regular boundary data g the existence of a variational solution to the gradient constrained problem in the sense of Definition 1.2 via the method of minimizing movements (finite time discretization). We assume that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$g\in \textrm{Lip}^x(\Omega _T)$$\end{document}g∈Lipx(ΩT) additionally satisfies4.1\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \partial _tg\in L^2(\Omega _T)\cap L^\infty \big ( 0,T; W^{1,\infty }(\Omega )\big ) \text{ and } g_o:=g(0)\in W^{1,\infty }(\Omega ). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}∂tg∈L2(ΩT)∩L∞(0,T;W1,∞(Ω))andgo:=g(0)∈W1,∞(Ω).This implies in particular that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$g\in C^0( [0,T], L^2(\Omega ))$$\end{document}g∈C0([0,T],L2(Ω)). Furthermore, let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L>0$$\end{document}L>0 such that4.2\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert \nabla g\Vert _{L^\infty (\Omega _T,\mathbb {R}^n)} < L. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖∇g‖L∞(ΩT,Rn)<L.

Proposition 4.1

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$f:\mathbb {R}^n\rightarrow \mathbb {R}$$\end{document}f:Rn→R be convex, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L>0$$\end{document}L>0 and suppose that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$g\in \textrm{Lip}^x(\Omega _T)$$\end{document}g∈Lipx(ΩT) satisfies (4.1) and (4.2). Then, there exists a unique variational solution u in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega _T$$\end{document}ΩT in the class \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textrm{Lip}^x_g(\Omega _T,L)$$\end{document}Lipgx(ΩT,L) in the sense of Definition 1.2. Moreover, we have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial _t u\in L^2(\Omega _T)$$\end{document}∂tu∈L2(ΩT).

The proof of Proposition 4.1 will be established in § 4.1–4.3.

A Sequence of Minimizers for Variational Functionals

We fix a step size \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$h\in (0,1]$$\end{document}h∈(0,1] and define for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$i\in \mathbb {N}_0$$\end{document}i∈N0 with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ih\le T$$\end{document}ih≤T the time-discretized boundary values \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$g_i:=g(ih)\in W^{1,\infty } (\Omega )$$\end{document}gi:=g(ih)∈W1,∞(Ω). Note that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert \nabla g_i\Vert _{L^\infty (\Omega ,\mathbb {R}^n)}\le L$$\end{document}‖∇gi‖L∞(Ω,Rn)≤L. Our goal is to inductively construct a sequence \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_i\in g_i + W^{1,\infty }_0(\Omega )$$\end{document}ui∈gi+W01,∞(Ω) of Lipschitz minimizers to certain elliptic variational functionals satisfying the gradient constraint \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert \nabla u_i\Vert _{L^\infty (\Omega ,\mathbb {R}^n)}\le L$$\end{document}‖∇ui‖L∞(Ω,Rn)≤L. The precise construction is as follows. Suppose that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_{i-1}\in g_{i-1}+ W^{1,\infty }_0(\Omega )$$\end{document}ui-1∈gi-1+W01,∞(Ω) for some \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$i\in \mathbb {N}$$\end{document}i∈N has already been defined. If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$i=1$$\end{document}i=1, then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_0=g_o$$\end{document}u0=go is the initial boundary datum. Then, we let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_i$$\end{document}ui be the minimizer of the variational functional\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \mathsf F_{i}[v] := \int _{\Omega }f(\nabla v)\,\textrm{d}x+\tfrac{1}{2h}\int _\Omega |v-u_{i-1}|^2\,\textrm{d}x\, . $$\end{document}Fi[v]:=∫Ωf(∇v)dx+12h∫Ω|v-ui-1|2dx.in the class of functions \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v\in g_i+ W^{1,\infty }_0(\Omega )$$\end{document}v∈gi+W01,∞(Ω) with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert \nabla v\Vert _{L^\infty (\Omega ,\mathbb {R}^n)}\le L$$\end{document}‖∇v‖L∞(Ω,Rn)≤L. Observe that this class in non-empty since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v=g_i$$\end{document}v=gi is admissible. Note also that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textsf{F}_{i}$$\end{document}Fi is bounded on this function class. The existence of a unique minimizer \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_i$$\end{document}ui can be deduced by means of standard compactness arguments (i.e. by use of the Arzela-Ascoli theorem) using the convexity of f. We note that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert \nabla u_i\Vert _{L^\infty (\Omega ,\mathbb {R}^n)}\le L$$\end{document}‖∇ui‖L∞(Ω,Rn)≤L for any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$i\in \mathbb {N}_0$$\end{document}i∈N0 with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ih\le T$$\end{document}ih≤T, by construction.

Minimizing Properties of the Approximation Sequences

use this minimality condition below to derive a variational inequality for the approximating functions \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} In this discrete variational inequality we can then proceed to the limit \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\ell \rightarrow \infty $$\end{document}ℓ→∞ and obtain that u is the desired solution. The precise procedure is as follows. We first re-write the above inequality in terms of the total energy of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u^{(h_\ell )}$$\end{document}u(hℓ) as follows\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \iint _{\Omega _\tau }&f\big (\nabla u^{(h_\ell )}\big ) \,\textrm{d}x\textrm{d}t\\&\le \iint _{\Omega _\tau }f(\nabla v) \,\textrm{d}x\textrm{d}t\\&\phantom {\le \,}+ \tfrac{1}{2h_\ell } \iint _{\Omega _\tau } \Big [\big |v(t)-u^{(h_\ell )}(t{-}h_\ell )\big |^2 -\big |u^{(h_\ell )}(t)- u^{(h_\ell )}(t{-}h_\ell )\big |^2\Big ]\,\textrm{d}x\textrm{d}t\\&= \iint (u^{(h_\ell )}-u^{(h_\ell )}(t{-}h_\ell )\big )\Big ]\,\textrm{d}x\textrm{d}t. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}∬Ωτf(∇u(hℓ))dxdt≤∬Ωτf(∇v)dxdt≤+12hℓ∬Ωτ[|v(t)-u(hℓ)(t-hℓ)|2-|u(hℓ)(t)-u(hℓ)(t-hℓ)|2]dxdt=∬Ωτf(∇v)dxdt≤+1hℓ∬Ωτ[12|v-u(hℓ)|2+(v-u(hℓ))(u(hℓ)-u(hℓ)(t-hℓ))]dxdt.We now replace \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u^{(h_\ell )}$$\end{document}u(hℓ) and a general admissible function v as in (4.14), i.e. the function For this, however, it is necessary that v is also defined for negative times. Therefore, we define \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v(t):=v(0)$$\end{document}v(t):=v(0) for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$t<0$$\end{document}t<0 and assume that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v(0)\in L^2(\Omega )$$\end{document}v(0)∈L2(Ω) exists. This allows us to conclude4.15\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \nonumber \iint _{\Omega _\tau }&f\big (\nabla u^{(h_\ell )} \big )\,\textrm{d}x\textrm{d}t\\\nonumber&\le \iint _{\Omega _\tau }f(\nabla v) \,\textrm{d}x\textrm{d}t+ \tfrac{1}{h_\ell } \iint _{\Omega _\tau } \big (v-u^{(h_\ell )}\big )\big (v-v(t-h_\ell )\big ) \,\textrm{d}x\textrm{d}t\\\nonumber&\quad + \tfrac{1}{2h_\ell } \iint _{\Omega _\tau } \Big [\big |v-u^{(h_\ell )}\big |^2(t-h_\ell ) - \big |v-u^{(h_\ell )}\big |^2\Big ] \,\textrm{d}x\textrm{d}t\\\nonumber&\quad - \tfrac{1}{2h_\ell } \iint _{\Omega _\tau } \big |v - v(t-h_\ell ) - u^{(h_\ell )} + u^{(h_\ell )}(t-h_\ell )\big |^2 \,\textrm{d}x\textrm{d}t\\\nonumber&\le \iint _{\Omega _\tau }f(\nabla v) \,\textrm{d}x\textrm{d}t+ \iint _{\Omega _\tau } \big (v-u^{(h_\ell )}\big )\Delta _{-h_\ell }v \,\textrm{d}x\textrm{d}t\\&\phantom {=\ }- \tfrac{1}{2h_\ell } \iint _{\Omega \times [\tau -h_\ell ,\tau ]} \big |v-u^{(h_\ell )}\big |^2 \,\textrm{d}x\textrm{d}t+ \tfrac{1}{2h_\ell } \iint _{\Omega \times [-h_\ell ,0]} |v-g_o|^2 \,\textrm{d}x\textrm{d}t\,. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}∬Ωτf(∇u(hℓ))dxdt≤∬Ωτf(∇v)dxdt+1hℓ∬Ωτ(v-u(hℓ))(v-v(t-hℓ))dxdt+12hℓ∬Ωτ[|v-u(hℓ)|2(t-hℓ)-|v-u(hℓ)|2]dxdt-12hℓ∬Ωτ|v-v(t-hℓ)-u(hℓ)+u(hℓ)(t-hℓ)|2dxdt≤∬Ωτf(∇v)dxdt+∬Ωτ(v-u(hℓ))Δ-hℓvdxdt=-12hℓ∬Ω×[τ-hℓ,τ]|v-u(hℓ)|2dxdt+12hℓ∬Ω×[-hℓ,0]|v-go|2dxdt.

Variational Inequality for the Limit Map

Our aim here is to perform the limit \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$h_\ell \downarrow 0$$\end{document}hℓ↓0 in (4.15). To this aim we need to replace the boundary condition \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v = g^{(h_\ell )}$$\end{document}v=g(hℓ) on the lateral boundary by the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$h_\ell $$\end{document}hℓ-independent condition \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v = g$$\end{document}v=g. Therefore we consider \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v\in \textrm{Lip}^x_g(\Omega _T,L)$$\end{document}v∈Lipgx(ΩT,L) satisfying \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial _tv\in L^2(\Omega _T)$$\end{document}∂tv∈L2(ΩT) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v(0)\in L^2( \Omega )$$\end{document}v(0)∈L2(Ω). Moreover, we extend v to negative times \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$t<0$$\end{document}t<0 by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v(t):=v( 0)\in L^2(\Omega )$$\end{document}v(t):=v(0)∈L2(Ω). In (4.15) we would like to insert \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v+g^{(h_\ell )}-g$$\end{document}v+g(hℓ)-g as comparison map. However, this is not possible due to the gradient constraint. This problem can be compensated by considering a convex combination of v and g instead of v. More precisely, for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda \in (0,1)$$\end{document}λ∈(0,1), we consider\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ v^{(h_\ell )} := \lambda g +(1-\lambda )v+g^{(h_\ell )}-g $$\end{document}v(hℓ):=λg+(1-λ)v+g(hℓ)-gas a comparison map. Note that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v^{(h_\ell )}\in g^{(h_\ell )} +L^\infty (0,T;W^{1,\infty }_0(\Omega ))$$\end{document}v(hℓ)∈g(hℓ)+L∞(0,T;W01,∞(Ω)). Moreover, in view of (4.2) we have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} Existence of Solutions to the Gradient Constrained Problem

In this section we prove the existence of a variational solution to the gradient constrained problem in the sense of Definition 1.2 under the assumptions of Theorem 1.3. In particular, we assume the boundary data g to admit merely the regularity given in hypothesis (1.2).

Proposition 5.1

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$f:\mathbb {R}^n\rightarrow \mathbb {R}$$\end{document}f:Rn→R be convex, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L>0$$\end{document}L>0 and suppose that g satisfies (1.2) and (1.5). Then, there exists a unique variational solution u in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega _T$$\end{document}ΩT in the class \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textrm{Lip}^x_g(\Omega _T,L)$$\end{document}Lipgx(ΩT,L) in the sense of Definition 1.2.

For the proof of Proposition 5.1 we will rely on the existence result from Proposition 4.1 for more regular boundary data. Therefore, we first regularize the boundary data in such a way that Proposition 4.1 is applicable. Subsequently we will show that the limit function is a solution to the original problem.

Construction of Regularized Data

The Regularized Problem Formulation

to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek}
