Using the bound (5.3) for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial _tg_i$$\end{document}∂tgi to estimate the second term on the right, we obtain that for any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$t_o\in (0,T]$$\end{document}to∈(0,T] that5.11\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \sup _{t\in [0,t_o]} \Vert (g_i-u_i)(t)\Vert ^2_{L^2(\Omega )} \le 4t_o\Vert \partial _tg\Vert ^2_{L^2(\Omega _{t_o})} + 4|\Omega _{t_o}|\sup _{|\xi |\le L} f(\xi ). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}supt∈[0,to]‖(gi-ui)(t)‖L2(Ω)2≤4to‖∂tg‖L2(Ωto)2+4|Ωto|sup|ξ|≤Lf(ξ).In particular for the choice \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$t_o=T$$\end{document}to=T, we obtain an \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^\infty {-}L^2$$\end{document}L∞-L2 estimate for the solutions \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_i$$\end{document}ui. Indeed, we have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \sup _{t\in [0,T]}\Vert u_i(t)\Vert ^2_{L^2(\Omega )} \le 2 \sup _{t\in [0,T]}\Vert g_i(t)\Vert ^2_{L^2(\Omega )} + 8T \Vert \partial _tg\Vert ^2_{L^2(\Omega _T)} + 8|\Omega _T| \sup _{|\xi |\le L} f(\xi ). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}supt∈[0,T]‖ui(t)‖L2(Ω)2≤2supt∈[0,T]‖gi(t)‖L2(Ω)2+8T‖∂tg‖L2(ΩT)2+8|ΩT|sup|ξ|≤Lf(ξ).The first term on the right-hand side is uniformly (with respect to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$i\in \mathbb {N}$$\end{document}i∈N) bounded with respect to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$i\in \mathbb {N}$$\end{document}i∈N according to Lemma 2.6 applied with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$r=\infty $$\end{document}r=∞ and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$X=L^2(\Omega )$$\end{document}X=L2(Ω). Therefore, the above estimate together with the uniform gradient bound \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert \nabla u_i\Vert _{L^\infty (\Omega _T,\mathbb {R}^n)}\le L$$\end{document}‖∇ui‖L∞(ΩT,Rn)≤L imply that the sequence \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(u_i)_{i\in \mathbb {N}}$$\end{document}(ui)i∈N is uniformly bounded in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^\infty \big ( 0,T;L^2(\Omega )\big )$$\end{document}L∞(0,T;L2(Ω)) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^\infty \big ( 0,T; W^{1,\infty }(\Omega )\big )$$\end{document}L∞(0,T;W1,∞(Ω)).

Limit Passage and Convergence

Due to the uniform estimates there exists a function\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ u\in L^{\infty }\big (0,T; L^2(\Omega )\big )\cap \bigcap _{q\ge 1}L^{q}\big (0,T;W^{1,p}(\Omega )\big ) $$\end{document}u∈L∞(0,T;L2(Ω))∩⋂q≥1Lq(0,T;W1,p(Ω))and a subsequence of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_{i}$$\end{document}ui (still denoted this way) such that5.12\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \left\{ \begin{array}{cl} u_{i}\rightharpoonup u & \text{ weakly } \text{ in } L^{q}\big (0,T; W^{1,q}(\Omega )\big ) \text{ for } \text{ any } q\ge 1,\\ u_{i}\rightharpoonup u & \text{ weakly}^* \text{ in } L^{\infty }\big (0,T, L^2(\Omega )\big ), \end{array}\right. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}ui⇀uweaklyinLq(0,T;W1,q(Ω))foranyq≥1,ui⇀uweakly∗inL∞(0,T,L2(Ω)),as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$i\rightarrow \infty $$\end{document}i→∞. At this point we need to show that u is the sought variational solution with lateral boundary values g and initial value \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$g_o$$\end{document}go, that satisfies the gradient constraint. The latter results from the weak \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^p$$\end{document}Lp-convergence of the gradients and the lower-semicontinuity of the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^q$$\end{document}Lq-norm with respect to weak convergence. More precisely, for any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$q\ge 1$$\end{document}q≥1 we haveso that the gradient constraint \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert \nabla u\Vert _{L^\infty (\Omega _T,\mathbb {R}^n)}\le L$$\end{document}‖∇u‖L∞(ΩT,Rn)≤L holds. In order to check that u solves the variational inequality we consider an arbitrary comparison function \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v\in \textrm{Lip}^x_g(\Omega _T,L)$$\end{document}v∈Lipgx(ΩT,L) with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial _tv\in L^2(\Omega )$$\end{document}∂tv∈L2(Ω) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v(0)\in L^2(\Omega )$$\end{document}v(0)∈L2(Ω). As before, we write \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v_i:=[v]_{h_i}$$\end{document}vi:=[v]hi for the time mollification of v and with initial value \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$g_o$$\end{document}go. As shown in § 5, the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v_i$$\end{document}vi are admissible in the variational inequality (5.10), from which we obtain5.13\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \tfrac{1}{2} \Vert (v_i-u_i)(\tau )\Vert ^2_{L^2(\Omega )}&+\iint _{\Omega _\tau } f(\nabla u_i)\,\textrm{d}x\textrm{d}t\nonumber \\&\le \iint _{\Omega _\tau }\partial _tv_i(v_i-u_i)\,\textrm{d}x\textrm{d}t+ \iint _{\Omega _\tau } f(\nabla v_i)\,\textrm{d}x\textrm{d}t\end{aligned}$$\end{document}12‖(vi-ui)(τ)‖L2(Ω)2+∬Ωτf(∇ui)dxdt≤∬Ωτ∂tvi(vi-ui)dxdt+∬Ωτf(∇vi)dxdtfor any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tau \in [0,T]$$\end{document}τ∈[0,T]. Note that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v_i$$\end{document}vi and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$g_i$$\end{document}gi attend the same initial datum \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$g_o$$\end{document}go, so that the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^2$$\end{document}L2-boundary term vanishes for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$t=0$$\end{document}t=0. Hereby, the degree of difficulty in the treatment of the individual terms is quite different. We start with the second integral on the right. This allows us to replace the second integral on the right-hand side of (5.13) by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\iint _{\Omega _\tau } f(\nabla v)\,\textrm{d}x\textrm{d}t$$\end{document}∬Ωτf(∇v)dxdt after passing to the limit \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$i\rightarrow \infty $$\end{document}i→∞. In the first integral, i.e. the integral containing the time derivative, we replace \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial _t v_i$$\end{document}∂tvi by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial _t\widetilde{v}_i$$\end{document}∂tv~i, where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widetilde{v}_i$$\end{document}v~i is defined in (5.7). This yields \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial _t\widetilde{v}_i\rightarrow \partial _tv$$\end{document}∂tv~i→∂tv in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^2(\Omega _T)$$\end{document}L2(ΩT) as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$i\rightarrow \infty $$\end{document}i→∞. Finally, by the dominated convergence theorem we can also pass to the limit \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$i\rightarrow \infty $$\end{document}i→∞ in the integral on the right-hand side containing the time derivative. Recall that for a.e. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tau $$\end{document}τ the functions \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tau \mapsto \iint _{\Omega _\tau }\partial _tv_i(v_i-u_i)\,\textrm{d}x\textrm{d}t$$\end{document}τ↦∬Ωτ∂tvi(vi-ui)dxdt are converging, i.e. on the one hand we have a.e. convergence on (0, T), and on the other hand, the functions are uniformly bounded with respect to i and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tau $$\end{document}τ. In this way we getfor any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$t_o\in [0,T]$$\end{document}to∈[0,T] and any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta \in (0,T-t_o]$$\end{document}δ∈(0,T-to]. Here we let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta \downarrow 0$$\end{document}δ↓0 and obtain\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \tfrac{1}{2} \Vert (v&-u)(t_o)\Vert ^2_{L^2(\Omega )} + \iint _{\Omega _{t_o}} f(\nabla u)\,\textrm{d}x\textrm{d}t\nonumber \\&\le \iint _{\Omega _{t_o}}\big [\partial _tv(v-u)+f(\nabla v)\big ]\textrm{d}x\textrm{d}t+\tfrac{1}{2} \Vert v(0)-g_o\Vert ^2_{L^2(\Omega )} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}12‖(v-u)(to)‖L2(Ω)2+∬Ωtof(∇u)dxdt≤∬Ωto[∂tv(v-u)+f(∇v)]dxdt+12‖v(0)-go‖L2(Ω)2for a.e. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$t_o\in [0,T]$$\end{document}to∈[0,T]. Overall, we have shown that u is a variational solution of the gradient constraint problem. The uniqueness can be deduced as in [9, Lemma 3.3]. This finishes the proof of Proposition 5.1.

Eliminating the Gradient Constraint

In this section we will remove the gradient constraint from the variational solutions constructed in Proposition 5.1. This will be achieved by showing that the gradient is bounded in terms of a constant depending only on the data. Throughout this section we assume all hypothesis of Theorem 1.3 to be in force.

Properties of the Integrand Function f

The following proposition contains some useful properties of f and its polar function (convex conjugate) \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$f^*:\mathbb {R}^n\rightarrow [-\infty ,\infty ]$$\end{document}f∗:Rn→[-∞,∞] defined by\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} f^*(\eta ):=\sup _{\xi \in \mathbb {R}^n}\big \{ \eta \cdot \xi -f(\xi )\big \}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}f∗(η):=supξ∈Rn{η·ξ-f(ξ)}.

there exists \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$c=c(\varepsilon )\in \mathbb {R}$$\end{document}c=c(ε)∈R such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} f(\xi )\ge \tfrac{1}{4} \varepsilon |\xi |^2 - c \quad \text{ for } \text{ any } \xi \in \mathbb {R}^n; \end{aligned}$$\end{document}f(ξ)≥14ε|ξ|2-cforanyξ∈Rn;

the restriction of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\nabla f$$\end{document}∇f to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {R}^n\setminus B_1$$\end{document}Rn\B1 is an invertible vector-field with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} (\nabla f)^{-1}=\nabla f^*. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}(∇f)-1=∇f∗.

Proof

Property (1) follows for large values of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|\xi |$$\end{document}|ξ| by Taylor’s expansion and the uniform convexity of f from (1.7). Property (2) is a straightforward consequence of the definition of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$f^*$$\end{document}f∗ taking into account that f is super-linear (cf. [20, Lemma 3.1]) and the assertion from (1). The proof of property (3) can be found in [19] for a special class of functions. The general case can be retrieved from [20, Lemma 3.3]. Property (4) is a consequence of [18, Chapter 1, Corollary 5.2] and the fact that f is a \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C^2$$\end{document}C2-function outside the unit ball by assumption. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

Construction of Barriers

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha \in \mathbb {R}\setminus \{0\}$$\end{document}α∈R\{0}. For \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$y:[0,T]\rightarrow \mathbb {R}^n$$\end{document}y:[0,T]→Rn and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$c:[0,T]\rightarrow \mathbb {R}$$\end{document}c:[0,T]→R we consider the following function:6.1\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} v(x,t) = \frac{n}{\alpha }f^*\Big (\frac{\alpha }{n}\big (x-y(t)\big )\Big )-c(t). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}v(x,t)=nαf∗(αn(x-y(t)))-c(t).In view of Proposition 6.1 (3), (4) it is easy to check that6.2\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} {{\,\textrm{div}\,}}_x \nabla _\xi f\big (\nabla v(x,t)\big )=\alpha , \end{aligned}$$\end{document}divx∇ξf(∇v(x,t))=α,provided that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Big |\frac{\alpha }{n}\big (x-y(t)\big )\Big | \ge r \quad \text{ and }\quad \Big |\nabla f^*\Big (\frac{\alpha }{n}\big (x-y(t)\big )\Big )\Big | \ge 1. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}|αn(x-y(t))|≥rand|∇f∗(αn(x-y(t)))|≥1.We recall that the class of functions in (6.1) was introduced by Cellina in [14] for the stationary case. In the same paper it was also shown that this class of functions has a property corresponding to (6.2) at the level of the associated variational integral, i.e. they minimize the variational integral that formally has (6.2) as Euler-Lagrange equation. For this, it was only necessary to assume that f is convex. Our goal is to show that for suitable choices of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha $$\end{document}α, y(t) and c(t) the function v can be used in the time dependent setting as a barrier function. We start with an auxiliary lemma.

Lemma 6.2

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha \in \mathbb {R}\setminus \{0\}$$\end{document}α∈R\{0} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$M,Q_1>0$$\end{document}M,Q1>0. For \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$b\in \mathbb {R}$$\end{document}b∈R and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$w\in \mathbb {R}^n$$\end{document}w∈Rn we define\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \widetilde{\Omega }_{b,w} := \left\{ x\in \mathbb {R}^n: \frac{n}{\alpha }f^*\left( \frac{\alpha }{n}\,x \right) -w\cdot x-b\le 0\right\} . $$\end{document}Ω~b,w:=x∈Rn:nαf∗αnx-w·x-b≤0.If\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ b > \min _{x\in \mathbb {R}^n} \Big [ \frac{n}{\alpha }f^*\left( \frac{\alpha }{n}\,x \right) -w\cdot x\Big ], $$\end{document}b>minx∈Rn[nαf∗αnx-w·x],then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widetilde{\Omega }_{b,w}$$\end{document}Ω~b,w is non-empty, bounded and convex. Furthermore, if6.3\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} b \ge \Gamma := \max _{x\in \overline{B_M(0)},\,w\in \overline{B_{Q_1}(0)} }\Big [ \frac{n}{\alpha }f^*\left( \frac{\alpha }{n}\,x \right) -w\cdot x\Big ], \end{aligned}$$\end{document}b≥Γ:=maxx∈BM(0)¯,w∈BQ1(0)¯[nαf∗αnx-w·x],then, for any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$w\in \overline{B_{Q_1}(0)}$$\end{document}w∈BQ1(0)¯ the ball \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\overline{B_M(0)}$$\end{document}BM(0)¯ is contained in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widetilde{\Omega }_{b,w}$$\end{document}Ω~b,w. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

Proof

The proof is straightforward. For the sake of completeness, however, we will elaborate on it. Since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$f^*$$\end{document}f∗ has superlinear growth and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {R}^n\ni x\mapsto w\cdot x$$\end{document}Rn∋x↦w·x is linear, we have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ k(x):=\frac{n}{\alpha }f^*\left( \frac{\alpha }{n}\,x \right) -w\cdot x\rightarrow \infty \quad \text{ as } |x|\rightarrow \infty . $$\end{document}k(x):=nαf∗αnx-w·x→∞as|x|→∞.This implies that k attains its minimum on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {R}^n$$\end{document}Rn. If b is larger than the minimal value of k, we obtain that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widetilde{\Omega }_{b,w}$$\end{document}Ω~b,w is non-empty and bounded. In addition, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widetilde{\Omega }_{b,w}$$\end{document}Ω~b,w is convex because it is constructed as a sublevel set of a convex function. This proves the first assertion of the lemma.

The second assertion, i.e. that (6.3) implies \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\overline{B_M(0)}\subset \widetilde{\Omega }_{b,w}$$\end{document}BM(0)¯⊂Ω~b,w, follows similarly. Fix \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$w\in \overline{B_{Q_1}(0)}$$\end{document}w∈BQ1(0)¯. Then k is bounded on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\overline{B_M(0)}$$\end{document}BM(0)¯. If b is greater than the maximum value of k on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\overline{B_M(0)}$$\end{document}BM(0)¯, then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\overline{B_M(0)}$$\end{document}BM(0)¯ is a subset of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widetilde{\Omega }_{b,w}$$\end{document}Ω~b,w, proving the second claim. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

Proposition 6.3

Let the assumptions of Theorem 1.3 be in force. Proof

We only prove the assertions for positive \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha $$\end{document}α, since the proof for the other case is analogous. We may assume \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha \ge \alpha _o\ge n$$\end{document}α≥αo≥n by choosing \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha _o\ge n$$\end{document}αo≥n.

Step 1: An auxiliary set. Let R be the radius from the R-uniformly convexity assumption (A1) on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega $$\end{document}Ω and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\nu _{x_o}$$\end{document}νxo the outward pointing unit vector associated with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$x_o\in \partial \Omega $$\end{document}xo∈∂Ω; see Definition 2.1. By \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varepsilon $$\end{document}ε and r we denote the corresponding parameters from (1.7) and Proposition 6.1 (3). Due to the super-linearity of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$f^*$$\end{document}f∗ there exists \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$M\ge r+{{\,\textrm{diam}\,}}(\Omega )$$\end{document}M≥r+diam(Ω), depending only on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varepsilon , R, {{\,\textrm{diam}\,}}(\Omega ), \nabla f, Q_1, \alpha $$\end{document}ε,R,diam(Ω),∇f,Q1,α, such that6.6\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} |\nabla f^*(\eta )|>\frac{\alpha R}{\varepsilon n}+Q_1\qquad \text{ for } \text{ any } \eta \in \mathbb {R}^n\setminus B_{M}(0). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}|∇f∗(η)|>αRεn+Q1foranyη∈Rn\BM(0).We define \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Gamma $$\end{document}Γ according to (6.3) from Lemma 6.2 for this particular choice of M. Note that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Gamma $$\end{document}Γ depends on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$n, \varepsilon , R, {{\,\textrm{diam}\,}}(\Omega ), \nabla f, Q_1, \alpha $$\end{document}n,ε,R,diam(Ω),∇f,Q1,α. Step 5: Proof of claim (4). With (6.12) and (6.9) we compute that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \nabla v(x,t)&= \nabla f^*\left( \frac{\alpha }{n}\big (x-y(t)\big )\right) \\&= \nabla f^*\left( \frac{\alpha }{n}\big (x-y(t)\big )\right) - Parabolic Sub- Super-Minimizers and the Comparison Principle

We recall that the variational solution constructed in Proposition 4.1 admits a time derivative \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial _tu\in L^2(\Omega _T)$$\end{document}∂tu∈L2(ΩT). Therefore, we may perform an integration by parts in the first term on the right-hand side of the variational inequality (1.6). In this way, the variational inequality can be re-written as\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \iint _{\Omega _T}f(\nabla u)\,\textrm{d}x\textrm{d}t&\le \iint _{\Omega _T}\big [\partial _{t}u(v-u) + f(\nabla v)\big ]\,\textrm{d}x\textrm{d}t\end{aligned}$$\end{document}∬ΩTf(∇u)dxdt≤∬ΩT[∂tu(v-u)+f(∇v)]dxdtfor any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v\in \textrm{Lip}^x_g(\Omega _T, L)$$\end{document}v∈Lipgx(ΩT,L) with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial _{t}v\in L^{2}(\Omega _{T})$$\end{document}∂tv∈L2(ΩT). Note that the assumption \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial _{t}v\in L^{2}(\Omega _{T})$$\end{document}∂tv∈L2(ΩT) can be eliminated by an approximation argument. This motivates the following definition:

Definition 6.4

(Parabolic sub-/super-minimizer) Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L\in (0,\infty ]$$\end{document}L∈(0,∞]. A map \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u\in \textrm{Lip}^x(\Omega _T, L)$$\end{document}u∈Lipx(ΩT,L) with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial _t u\in L^2(\Omega _T)$$\end{document}∂tu∈L2(ΩT) is called parabolic sub-minimizer (of the gradient constrained obstacle problem in the case \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L<\infty $$\end{document}L<∞) in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textrm{Lip}^x(\Omega _T, L)$$\end{document}Lipx(ΩT,L) if and only if6.21\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \iint _{\Omega _T}f(\nabla u)\,\textrm{d}x\textrm{d}t&\le \iint _{\Omega _T}\big [\partial _{t}u(v-u) + f(\nabla v)\big ]\,\textrm{d}x\textrm{d}t\end{aligned}$$\end{document}∬ΩTf(∇u)dxdt≤∬ΩT[∂tu(v-u)+f(∇v)]dxdtholds true for any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v\in \textrm{Lip}^x_u(\Omega _T, L)$$\end{document}v∈Lipux(ΩT,L) with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v\le u$$\end{document}v≤u in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega _T$$\end{document}ΩT. Moreover, a map \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u\in \textrm{Lip}^x(\Omega _T, L)$$\end{document}u∈Lipx(ΩT,L) is called parabolic super-minimizer in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textrm{Lip}^x(\Omega _T, L)$$\end{document}Lipx(ΩT,L) if and only if (6.21) holds true for any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v\in \textrm{Lip}^x_u(\Omega _T, L)$$\end{document}v∈Lipux(ΩT,L) with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v\ge u$$\end{document}v≥u in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega _T$$\end{document}ΩT. Finally, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u\in \textrm{Lip}^x(\Omega _T, L)$$\end{document}u∈Lipx(ΩT,L) is called parabolic minimizer if and only if (6.21) holds true for any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v\in \textrm{Lip}^x_u(\Omega _T, L)$$\end{document}v∈Lipux(ΩT,L).

The concept of parabolic minimizers for vector-valued integrands with quadratic growth originates from the work of Wieser [43]. Subsequently, it will be essential to establish that a localization principle with respect to the spatial variables holds for parabolic minimizers

Remark 6.5

(Localization in space) Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L\in (0,\infty )$$\end{document}L∈(0,∞) and suppose that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega '\subset \Omega $$\end{document}Ω′⊂Ω is an open, convex set, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$f:\mathbb {R}^n\rightarrow \mathbb {R}$$\end{document}f:Rn→R a convex integrand and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u\in \textrm{Lip}^x(\Omega _T, L)$$\end{document}u∈Lipx(ΩT,L) with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial _t u\in L^2(\Omega _T)$$\end{document}∂tu∈L2(ΩT) a parabolic minimizer in the sense of Definition 6.4 in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega _T$$\end{document}ΩT. Then, u is also a parabolic minimizer in the subcylinder \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega '_T:=\Omega '\times [0,T)$$\end{document}ΩT′:=Ω′×[0,T). The proof of this elementary fact is analogous to the setting of time independent boundary data and can be found in [6, Remark 4.2].

In the next lemma, we establish the comparison principle for parabolic sub- and super-minimizers.

Lemma 6.6

(Comparison principle) Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L\in (0,\infty ]$$\end{document}L∈(0,∞] and suppose that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega \subset \mathbb {R}^n$$\end{document}Ω⊂Rn is a bounded open set, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$f:\mathbb {R}^n\rightarrow \mathbb {R}$$\end{document}f:Rn→R is a convex integrand and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u, \tilde{u}\in \textrm{Lip}^x(\Omega _T, L)$$\end{document}u,u~∈Lipx(ΩT,L) with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial _t u,\partial _t\tilde{u}\in L^2(\Omega _T)$$\end{document}∂tu,∂tu~∈L2(ΩT). Suppose that u is a sub-minimizer and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tilde{u}$$\end{document}u~ is a super-minimizer in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega _T$$\end{document}ΩT in the sense of Definition 6.4 and that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u\le \tilde{u}$$\end{document}u≤u~ on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial _{\mathcal {P}}\Omega _T$$\end{document}∂PΩT. Then, we have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ u\le \tilde{u}\quad \text{ a.e. } \text{ in } \Omega _T. $$\end{document}u≤u~a.e.inΩT.

Proof

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tau \in (0,T]$$\end{document}τ∈(0,T]. We define that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} v:= \left\{ \begin{array}{cl} \min \{u, \tilde{u}\}, & \text{ in } \Omega _\tau , \\ u, & \text{ in } \Omega \times [\tau ,T), \end{array}\right. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}v:=min{u,u~},inΩτ,u,inΩ×[τ,T),and\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} w:= \left\{ \begin{array}{cl} \max \{u, \tilde{u}\}, & \text{ in } \Omega _\tau , \\ \tilde{u}, & \text{ in } \Omega \times [\tau ,T). \end{array}\right. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}w:=max{u,u~},inΩτ,u~,inΩ×[τ,T).We note that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v\in \textrm{Lip}^x_u(\Omega _T, L)$$\end{document}v∈Lipux(ΩT,L) with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v\le u$$\end{document}v≤u and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v(0)=u_o$$\end{document}v(0)=uo and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$w\in \textrm{Lip}^x_{\tilde{u}}(\Omega _T, L)$$\end{document}w∈Lipu~x(ΩT,L) with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$w\ge \tilde{u}$$\end{document}w≥u~ and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$w(0)=\tilde{u}_o$$\end{document}w(0)=u~o. This ensures that v is an admissible comparison function in the variational inequality (6.21) for u and w for of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tilde{u}$$\end{document}u~. Adding the two resulting inequalities and taking into account that the parts of the integrals on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega \times (\tau ,T)$$\end{document}Ω×(τ,T) cancel themselves out, we obtain6.22\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}&\iint _{\Omega _\tau }\big [f(\nabla u) + f(\nabla \tilde{u})\big ] \,\textrm{d}x\textrm{d}t\nonumber \\&\qquad \le \iint _{\Omega _\tau }\big [f(\nabla v) + f(\nabla w) + \partial _t u (v-u) + \partial _t \tilde{u}(w-\tilde{u})\big ]\,\textrm{d}x\textrm{d}t. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}∬Ωτ[f(∇u)+f(∇u~)]dxdt≤∬Ωτ[f(∇v)+f(∇w)+∂tu(v-u)+∂tu~(w-u~)]dxdt.We now consider the terms on the right-hand side of (6.22). From the definition of v and w we infer\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \iint _{\Omega _\tau }\big [f(\nabla v) + f(\nabla w)\big ] \,\textrm{d}x\textrm{d}t= \iint _{\Omega _\tau }\big [f(\nabla u) + f(\nabla \tilde{u})\big ] \,\textrm{d}x\textrm{d}t. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}∬Ωτ[f(∇v)+f(∇w)]dxdt=∬Ωτ[f(∇u)+f(∇u~)]dxdt.Moreover, we observe that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v-u=-(u-\tilde{u})_+$$\end{document}v-u=-(u-u~)+ and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$w-\tilde{u}=(u-\tilde{u})_+$$\end{document}w-u~=(u-u~)+ in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega _\tau $$\end{document}Ωτ, so that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \partial _t u(v-u) + \partial _t \tilde{u}(w-\tilde{u})&= -\partial _t (u-\tilde{u})(u-\tilde{u})_+ = -\tfrac{1}{2}\partial _t (u-\tilde{u})_+^2 . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}∂tu(v-u)+∂tu~(w-u~)=-∂t(u-u~)(u-u~)+=-12∂t(u-u~)+2.This implies\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \iint _{\Omega _\tau } \big [\partial _t u(v-u) + \partial _t \tilde{u}(w-\tilde{u})\big ] \textrm{d}x\textrm{d}t&= -\tfrac{1}{2}\iint _{\Omega _\tau } \partial _t (u-\tilde{u})_+^2 \textrm{d}x\textrm{d}t\\&= -\tfrac{1}{2} \int _{\Omega \times \{\tau \}} (u-\tilde{u})_+^2 \textrm{d}x. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}∬Ωτ[∂tu(v-u)+∂tu~(w-u~)]dxdt=-12∬Ωτ∂t(u-u~)+2dxdt=-12∫Ω×{τ}(u-u~)+2dx.Here we used the assumption that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(u-\tilde{u})_+(0)=0$$\end{document}(u-u~)+(0)=0. Joining the preceding identities with (6.22), we conclude\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \int _{\Omega \times \{\tau \}}(u - \tilde{u})_+^2\textrm{d}x\le 0 . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}∫Ω×{τ}(u-u~)+2dx≤0.Since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tau \in (0,T]$$\end{document}τ∈(0,T] was arbitrary, this proves the claim \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u\le \tilde{u}$$\end{document}u≤u~ a.e. in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega _T$$\end{document}ΩT. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

As a consequence of the preceding comparison principle, we obtain the following result:

Lemma 6.7

(Maximum principle) Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L\in (0,\infty ]$$\end{document}L∈(0,∞] and suppose that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega \subset \mathbb {R}^n$$\end{document}Ω⊂Rn is open and bounded, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$f:\mathbb {R}^n\rightarrow \mathbb {R}$$\end{document}f:Rn→R convex and let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u, \tilde{u}\in \textrm{Lip}^x(\Omega _T, L)$$\end{document}u,u~∈Lipx(ΩT,L) with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial _t u,\partial _t\tilde{u}\in L^2(\Omega _T)$$\end{document}∂tu,∂tu~∈L2(ΩT). Suppose that u is a sub-minimizer and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tilde{u}$$\end{document}u~ is a super-minimizer in the sense of Definition 6.4 in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega _T$$\end{document}ΩT. Then, we have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \sup _{\Omega _T}(u-\tilde{u})= \sup _{\partial _{\mathcal {P}}\Omega _{T}}(u-\tilde{u}). $$\end{document}supΩT(u-u~)=sup∂PΩT(u-u~).

Proof

For \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(x,t)\in \partial _{\mathcal {P}}\Omega _T$$\end{document}(x,t)∈∂PΩT it holds that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ u(x,t) = \tilde{u}(x,t) + u(x,t) - \tilde{u}(x,t) \le \tilde{u}(x,t) + \sup _{\partial _{\mathcal {P}}\Omega _T}(u - \tilde{u}). $$\end{document}u(x,t)=u~(x,t)+u(x,t)-u~(x,t)≤u~(x,t)+sup∂PΩT(u-u~).Since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tilde{u}$$\end{document}u~ is a parabolic super-minimizer in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega _T$$\end{document}ΩT it immediately follows that also \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tilde{u}+\sup _{\partial _{\mathcal {P}}\Omega _T}(u - \tilde{u})$$\end{document}u~+sup∂PΩT(u-u~) is parabolic super-minimizer in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega _T$$\end{document}ΩT. In view of Lemma 6.6 we therefore have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ u \le \tilde{u}+\sup _{\partial _{\mathcal {P}}\Omega _T}(u - \tilde{u}) \quad \text{ in } \Omega _T. $$\end{document}u≤u~+sup∂PΩT(u-u~)inΩT.This inequality can be re-written in the form\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \sup _{\Omega _T}(u - \tilde{u}) \le \sup _{\partial _{\mathcal {P}}\Omega _T}(u - \tilde{u}). $$\end{document}supΩT(u-u~)≤sup∂PΩT(u-u~).Since the reversed inequality holds trivially, this proves the claim. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

A Quantitative Bound on the Lipschitz Continuity

In this subsection, we establish a quantitative Lipschitz bound for parabolic minimizers of the gradient constraint problem under the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$t-\textrm{BSC}_Q$$\end{document}t-BSCQ.

Proposition 6.8

Let the assumptions of Theorem 1.3 be in force and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L\in (0,\infty ]$$\end{document}L∈(0,∞]. Then, every parabolic minimizer \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u\in \textrm{Lip}^x(\Omega _T, L)$$\end{document}u∈Lipx(ΩT,L) with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial _t u\in L^2(\Omega _T)$$\end{document}∂tu∈L2(ΩT) in the sense of Definition 6.4 and Cauchy-Dirichlet boundary datum g satisfies the gradient bound\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \Vert \nabla u\Vert _{L^\infty (\Omega _T,\mathbb {R}^n)} \le C, $$\end{document}‖∇u‖L∞(ΩT,Rn)≤C,where C depends on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$n, \varepsilon , R, {{\,\textrm{diam}\,}}(\Omega ), f, \nabla f, \Vert D^2f\Vert _{L^\infty (\mathbb {R}^n\setminus B_1)}, Q, [g]_{0,1;\Omega _T}, \mathsf Q$$\end{document}n,ε,R,diam(Ω),f,∇f,‖D2f‖L∞(Rn\B1),Q,[g]0,1;ΩT,Q

Proof

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$x_1\not = x_2$$\end{document}x1≠x2 two arbitrary points in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega $$\end{document}Ω and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$t\in (0,T)$$\end{document}t∈(0,T). Then, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_y$$\end{document}uy is a parabolic minimizer in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widetilde{\Omega }_T$$\end{document}Ω~T in the class \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textrm{Lip}^x(\widetilde{\Omega }_T, L)$$\end{document}Lipx(Ω~T,L) in the sense of Definition 6.4. We denote the intersection of both cylinders by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\Omega \cap \widetilde{\Omega })_T:=(\Omega \cap \widetilde{\Omega })\times (0,T)$$\end{document}(Ω∩Ω~)T:=(Ω∩Ω~)×(0,T). Lemma 6.5 ensures that both, u and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_y$$\end{document}uy are parabolic minimizers in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\Omega \cap \widetilde{\Omega })_T$$\end{document}(Ω∩Ω~)T in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textrm{Lip}^x((\Omega \cap \widetilde{\Omega })_T, L)$$\end{document}Lipx((Ω∩Ω~)T,L). Therefore, from the maximum principle in Lemma 6.7 we conclude that there exists a boundary point \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(x_o,t_o)\in \partial _{\mathcal {P}}\big ((\Omega \cap \widetilde{\Omega })_T\big )$$\end{document}(xo,to)∈∂P((Ω∩Ω~)T) such that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} |u(x_1,t)-u_y(x_1,t)| \le |u(x_o,t_o)-u_y(x_o,t_o)|. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}|u(x1,t)-uy(x1,t)|≤|u(xo,to)-uy(xo,to)|.In view of the definition of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_y$$\end{document}uy, this inequality yields6.23\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} |u(x_1,t)-u(x_2,t)| \le |u(x_o,t_o)-u(x_o+y,t_o)|. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}|u(x1,t)-u(x2,t)|≤|u(xo,to)-u(xo+y,to)|.Since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(x_o,t_o)\in \partial _{\mathcal {P}}\big ((\Omega \cap \widetilde{\Omega })_T\big )$$\end{document}(xo,to)∈∂P((Ω∩Ω~)T), we either have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek}
